# Scenarios

## crashlooping_pod

Alert fires for repeated container restarts.
RCA: the pod crashes at startup because the required environment
variable DEPLOY_ENV is missing from the deployment.
Expected: add the DEPLOY_ENV environment variable to the container
spec.

**Tags**: `difficulty_normal`, `core`, `classic`, `alert`

### Query

```
Orders are not being processed in warehouse-ops. Can you investigate what is wrong?
```

<a id="openai-gpt-5-4--crashlooping_pod"></a>

### openai-gpt-5-4 (run 1/3)

**Correctness**: ✅ PASS (score: 1.00)

> Custom answer correctness: 1.00 - The response is fully correct and aligns exactly with the expected response. It identifies the same root cause: the `order-fulfillment-daemon` pod in `warehouse-ops` is crash-looping because the required `DEPLOY_ENV` environment variable is missing from the deployment. It also provides supporting evidence consistent with that conclusion, including pod restart status, deployment availability, logs showing `Environment variable DEPLOY_ENV is undefined`, and the container startup logic that exits when the variable is absent.

**Duration**: 18s

**Tokens**: in 14,093 out 560

````markdown
Issue is in `warehouse-ops` itself: the only processing pod is crash-looping because a required environment variable is missing.

**Evidence**
- Active alert:
  - `WarehouseOpsPodRestarting`
  - pod: `warehouse-ops/order-fulfillment-daemon-d7549fc8b-xx96k`
- Pod state:
  - `order-fulfillment-daemon-d7549fc8b-xx96k`
  - `READY 0/1`, `STATUS Error`, `RESTARTS 4`
- Deployment state:
  - `Deployment/order-fulfillment-daemon`
  - `READY 0/1`, `AVAILABLE 0`
- Pod log:
  - `Environment variable DEPLOY_ENV is undefined`
- Deployment spec confirms the container exits when that variable is absent:
  - container `fulfillment-container` runs:
    - `if [ -z "${DEPLOY_ENV}" ]; then`
    - `echo "Environment variable DEPLOY_ENV is undefined"`
    - `exit 1`
- The deployment was created very recently:
  - `Deployment/order-fulfillment-daemon` created at `2026-09-28T20:45:37Z`
  - event: `Scaled up replica set order-fulfillment-daemon-d7549fc8b from 0 to 1`
- In `warehouse-ops`, there are no application ConfigMaps present that would supply this value:
  - only `kube-root-ca.crt` and `openshift-service-ca.crt`

**Root cause**
1. `Deployment/order-fulfillment-daemon` was deployed without `DEPLOY_ENV`.
2. The container is coded to exit immediately if `DEPLOY_ENV` is unset.
3. Result: no healthy fulfillment pod is running, so orders are not being processed.

**Fix / mitigation**
Add `DEPLOY_ENV` to `Deployment/order-fulfillment-daemon`, either:
- directly as an env var, or
- from a ConfigMap/Secret if that is how it should be managed.

Example fix in the deployment:
```yaml
spec:
  template:
    spec:
      containers:
      - name: fulfillment-container
        env:
        - name: DEPLOY_ENV
          value: "prod"
```

**Recommended next step**
- Patch/redeploy `Deployment/order-fulfillment-daemon` with the correct `DEPLOY_ENV` value.
- Once updated, verify a new pod becomes `Ready 1/1` and stops restarting.

If you want, I can apply the deployment fix for you once you confirm the intended `DEPLOY_ENV` value.
````

I found the failure.

### Evidence
- **Affected workload:** `Deployment/warehouse-ops/order-fulfillment-daemon`
  - `READY 0/1`, `AVAILABLE 0`
- **Affected pod:** `warehouse-ops/order-fulfillment-daemon-d7549fc8b-xx96k`
  - Status: `0/1`, `Error`
  - Restarts: `4`
- **Active alert:** `WarehouseOpsPodRestarting`
  - Pod: `order-fulfillment-daemon-d7549fc8b-xx96k`
  - Container: `fulfillment-container`
- **Pod log output:**
  - `Environment variable DEPLOY_ENV is undefined`
- **Pod spec / deployment spec:**
  - Container command explicitly exits if `DEPLOY_ENV` is missing:
    ```sh
    if [ -z "${DEPLOY_ENV}" ]; then
      echo "Environment variable DEPLOY_ENV is undefined"
      exit 1
    fi
    ```
  - The deployment has **no `env:` or `envFrom:` configured** for that container.
- **Events:**
  - `Back-off restarting failed container fulfillment-container`
  - Pod was created at `2026-09-28T20:45:37Z`
  - Latest failed start shown at `2026-09-28T20:47:05Z`

### Root cause
`order-fulfillment-daemon` is crashing because the required environment variable **`DEPLOY_ENV`** is not set in the deployment.

This is the direct reason orders are not being processed in `warehouse-ops`: the fulfillment worker is never staying up.

### Fix / mitigation
Set `DEPLOY_ENV` on `Deployment/warehouse-ops/order-fulfillment-daemon`, either:
1. directly in `spec.template.spec.containers[0].env`, or
2. via `envFrom` from the intended `ConfigMap`/`Secret`.

Example shape:
```yaml
env:
- name: DEPLOY_ENV
  value: "<expected-value>"
```

### Important note
I can confirm **the variable is missing**, but I cannot determine the **correct value** from the cluster resources I inspected. That value must come from the application’s intended configuration.

### Summary
- **Cause:** missing required env var `DEPLOY_ENV`
- **Impact:** `order-fulfillment-daemon` crash-looping, so fulfillment is not running
- **Exact failing pod:** `order-fulfillment-daemon-d7549fc8b-xx96k`

If you want, I can next inspect the deployment history or related GitOps/operator-managed resources to determine where this bad rollout came from.

````markdown
## Diagnosis: orders are blocked by two independent failures

### 1. **Warehouse fulfillment worker is unavailable** — direct blocker
**Evidence**
- `warehouse-ops/order-fulfillment-daemon` has **0/1 available** replicas.
- Its only pod, `order-fulfillment-daemon-d7549fc8b-xx96k`, has restarted **4 times** and is not Ready.
- The container exits with code `1`; its startup command explicitly requires `DEPLOY_ENV`.
- Previous container log:
  ```text
  Environment variable DEPLOY_ENV is undefined
  ```

**Root cause**
`DEPLOY_ENV` is absent from the `order-fulfillment-daemon` Deployment’s container environment. The process intentionally exits when it is unset, so no fulfillment worker is running.

**Fix**
Update `warehouse-ops/order-fulfillment-daemon` to provide the required `DEPLOY_ENV` value—preferably through a ConfigMap or Secret appropriate to the intended environment—then confirm the replacement pod becomes `1/1 Ready`.

---

### 2. **Payment processing is failing because PostgreSQL is connection-exhausted** — downstream order-processing blocker
**Evidence**
- Active critical alert: `PaymentErrorRateHigh`; error rate is **100%**.
- `payments/payments-api-559656657c-9w6z2` returns HTTP `503` for payment requests, with:
  ```text
  FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute
  ```
- PostgreSQL is configured with only `max_connections = 20`.
- Live database inspection found **23 connections**, including **16** from `dbuser` at `10.128.2.130`—the IP of:
  `shared-services/reporting-service-6d7f67656f-6s2cd`
- Those 16 connections are all `idle in transaction`.
- Reporting service logs show a repeating failure every 10 seconds:
  ```text
  INFO Open db connection
  ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero
  ```
  This behavior creates connections but leaves failed transactions open until PostgreSQL capacity is exhausted.

**Root cause**
`shared-services/reporting-service` has a defect in its pending-report calculation (`division by zero`) and does not clean up its database transactions/connections after failures. It consumes the limited PostgreSQL connection capacity, preventing `payments-api` from connecting. Payments then fail, blocking payment-dependent orders.

**Immediate mitigation — reversible**
1. Scale `shared-services/reporting-service` to zero to terminate its leaked sessions and free connections.
2. Verify `payments-api` stops returning `503` and payment success returns.
3. Restore reporting only after correcting its divide-by-zero handling and ensuring transaction/connection cleanup (or after deploying a validated fixed image).

**Permanent correction**
- Fix the reporting query/application logic that divides by zero.
- Ensure error paths issue a rollback and close/release the database connection.
- Review PostgreSQL connection limits/pooling after the leak is fixed; increasing `max_connections` alone would only defer recurrence.

---

### Related, but not the primary payment outage
`shared-services/reconciliation-service-587768b96f-wwg68` is also in `CrashLoopBackOff` with **97 restarts**. Its probes use **HTTP** on port `8443`, while the RHEL httpd container serves TLS there, producing probe responses `400`. This leaves reconciliation unavailable, but the evidence above directly explains the fulfillment and payment blockage.
````

## Root Cause: Missing `DEPLOY_ENV` Environment Variable

**Affected Resource:** Deployment `order-fulfillment-daemon` in namespace `warehouse-ops`

## failed_job

Batch job fails after exhausting retries.
RCA: the job cannot connect to its database at prod-db:3333; logs
show repeated TCP connection refused (errno 111). The database
service is either missing or misconfigured.
Expected: identify the connection failure as root cause and
recommend fixing the database endpoint.

**Tags**: `classic`, `core`, `difficulty_normal`

### Query

```
The latest inventory synchronization in catalog-mgmt failed. Can you find out why?
```

<a id="openai-gpt-5-4--failed_job"></a>

### openai-gpt-5-4 (run 1/3)

**Correctness**: ✅ PASS (score: 0.98)

> Custom answer correctness: 0.98 - The response is essentially correct and strongly aligned with the expected answer. It identifies the key failure cause as the `inventory-sync-validator` job being unable to connect to `prod-db:3333`, which matches the expected response exactly.

**Duration**: 32s

**Tokens**: in 120,386 out 575

````markdown
**Why the latest inventory synchronization failed**

**Evidence**
- The failing workload is `Job` **`catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-validator`**.
- Job status:
  - `Failed`
  - `failed: 2`
  - condition: `BackoffLimitExceeded`
- Failed pods:
  - `catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-validator-bhrqk`
  - `catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-validator-kl66r`

**Pod logs from both failed runs**
- `Resolved endpoint prod-db:3333 via service discovery`
- `Socket connect returned errno 111 - connection refused`
- `Retry 4 of 4 exhausted`
- `FATAL: Unable to connect to required database`

**Cluster state**
- In namespace `catalog-mgmt`, there is **no `Service/prod-db`**
- In namespace `catalog-mgmt`, there is **no `Endpoints/prod-db`**

**ConfigMap content used by the job**
- `ConfigMap` **`catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-entrypoint`** contains:
  - `DB_HOST = "prod-db"`
  - `DB_PORT = 3333`
  - code that exits with `sys.exit(1)` after 4 failed attempts

**Related event**
- First pod had a transient mount issue:
  - `MountVolume.SetUp failed for volume "scripts" : configmap "inventory-sync-entrypoint" not found`
- But that was not the final blocker, because the retry pod started successfully and failed again on the same DB connection error.

## Root cause
The latest sync failed because the job tried to connect to **`prod-db:3333`** and the connection was refused on every retry. In addition, there is no `prod-db` Service or Endpoints object in `catalog-mgmt`, so the configured database target is not backed by a Kubernetes service in that namespace.

## Fix / mitigation
1. **Correct the database target**
   - Verify the intended DB hostname and port for inventory sync.
   - Update `ConfigMap/catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-entrypoint` if `prod-db:3333` is wrong.

2. **Create or restore the missing service/endpoints**
   - If the DB should be exposed inside `catalog-mgmt`, create/fix `Service/prod-db` and ensure it has ready endpoints.

3. **Rerun the job after fixing connectivity**
   - The current job already hit `BackoffLimitExceeded`; it will not recover on its own.

## Bottom line
The failure is **not** due to pod scheduling or OpenShift security policy. The sync job is failing because it cannot reach its configured database target **`prod-db:3333`**, and there is currently no matching Kubernetes service/endpoints in `catalog-mgmt`.
````

````markdown
Yes. The latest run failed because the job could not connect to its configured database endpoint.

**Evidence**
- Failed job: `catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-validator`
- Start time: `2026-09-28T20:48:45Z`
- Failure reason on the Job:
  - `BackoffLimitExceeded`
  - `Job has reached the specified backoff limit`
- Failed pods:
  1. `catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-validator-bhrqk`
  2. `catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-validator-kl66r`

**Pod logs from both failed attempts**
- `Resolved endpoint prod-db:3333 via service discovery`
- `Opening TCP socket to prod-db:3333`
- `Socket connect returned errno 111 - connection refused`
- `All connection attempts failed after 4 retries`
- `FATAL: Unable to connect to required database`

**Configuration causing it**
- The mounted script in ConfigMap `catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-entrypoint` hard-codes:
  - `DB_HOST = "prod-db"`
  - `DB_PORT = 3333`

**Why this is the root cause**
1. The container started successfully and ran the script.
2. Both retries failed for the same reason: TCP connection refused to `prod-db:3333`.
3. There is **no `Service` named `prod-db`** in the cluster resources I checked, including `catalog-mgmt` and other namespaces.
4. The only obvious database service present is `shared-services/postgres` on port `5432`, not `3333`.

**Secondary observation**
- There was an early event on pod `inventory-sync-validator-bhrqk`:
  - `MountVolume.SetUp failed for volume "scripts" : configmap "inventory-sync-entrypoint" not found`
- But that was transient. The ConfigMap was created immediately after, the container started, and the actual fatal error was the database connection refusal.

**Conclusion**
The latest inventory synchronization failed because it was configured to use **`prod-db:3333`**, and that endpoint is not valid/reachable in this cluster. The job exhausted its retries and failed.

**Fix / mitigation**
- Update the inventory sync configuration to point to the real database service and port.
- Based on current cluster state, the likely intended target is:
  - Service: `shared-services/postgres`
  - Port: `5432`
- Also ensure the ConfigMap or environment injection is created **before** the job starts, to avoid the transient mount error.

## Diagnosis

...

```

The configured endpoint is hard-coded in ConfigMap **`catalog-mgmt/inventory-sync-entrypoint`**:

```text

```

```text

configmap "inventory-sync-entrypoint" not found
```

The ConfigMap was created shortly afterward at `20:48:46 UTC`, and both pods subsequently started. This was a startup race, but it was **not the final cause** of the synchronization failure.

## Root cause

The inventory validator is configured to connect to `prod-db:3333`, but the corresponding database Service/backend is absent or not listening on that port. The connection was actively refused on every retry.

## Recommended fix

1. Restore or create the expected `prod-db` Service in `catalog-mgmt`.
2. Ensure it selects healthy database pods with a target port listening on **3333**.
3. If the database moved, update `inventory-sync-entrypoint` to the correct hostname and port.
4. After validating connectivity, rerun the failed Job.

The ConfigMap should also be provisioned before creating the Job to avoid the observed volume-mount race.

  It contains no socket or database client call. It prints the failure for every one of its four retry iterations, then calls `sys.exit(1)`.

## Root Cause: No Database Service Exists

The Job **`inventory-sync-validator`** in namespace **`catalog-mgmt`** failed because it cannot connect to its database dependency.

### Evidence

1. **Both pod attempts failed identically** — pods `inventory-sync-validator-bhrqk` and `inventory-sync-validator-kl66r` both exited with code **1** after exhausting all 4 connection retries to `prod-db:3333` with **`errno 111 — connection refused`**.

2. **No `prod-db` Service exists** — there is no Service named `prod-db` in the `catalog-mgmt` namespace (and no Services or Endpoints exist in this namespace at all). DNS may resolve the name (if it exists elsewhere), but nothing is listening on port **3333**.

3. **Job hit its backoff limit** — the Job is configured with `backoffLimit: 1`, so after 2 failed pods it entered `BackoffLimitExceeded` and marked itself as **Failed**.

4. **Secondary issue (non-blocking)** — the first pod (`bhrqk`) logged a warning: `MountVolume.SetUp failed for volume "scripts": configmap "inventory-sync-entrypoint" not found`. This was a transient race condition — the ConfigMap was created at nearly the same time as the Job. The volume eventually mounted and the script ran, so this did not cause the failure.

The database backend that the job expects at **`prod-db:3333`** is missing. To resolve this:

1. **Create the database Service and backing workload** — deploy the database (or whatever backend `prod-db` represents) in the `catalog-mgmt` namespace with a Service named `prod-db` exposing port **3333**. If the database lives in a different namespace, use a fully-qualified DNS name (e.g., `prod-db.<other-namespace>.svc.cluster.local`) or create an [ExternalName Service](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/services-networking/service/#externalname) pointing to it.

2. **Re-run the Job** — since the Job has permanently failed, delete it and recreate it:
   ```
   oc delete job inventory-sync-validator -n catalog-mgmt
   oc create -f <job-manifest>.yaml -n catalog-mgmt
   ```

3. **Consider increasing `backoffLimit`** — the current limit of `1` (2 total attempts) is very aggressive. If transient database unavailability is expected, raising this value would improve resilience.

## failing_api_alert_cross_namespace

Alert fires for 100% payment error rate (payments-api returns
503).
RCA: reporting-service in shared-services leaks database connections,
exhausting the shared PostgreSQL pool; payments-api in payments cannot
acquire a connection.
Expected: identify shared-services/reporting-service as the root cause;
shared-services/reconciliation-service CrashLooping is a separate issue,
not the cause.

**Tags**: `difficulty_hard`, `core`, `classic`, `alert`

### Query

```
The payment service in the payments namespace is failing customer requests. Can you investigate the cause and check its dependencies in other namespaces?
```

<a id="openai-gpt-5-4--failing_api_alert_cross_namespace"></a>

### openai-gpt-5-4 (run 1/3)

**Correctness**: ✅ PASS (score: 0.98)

> Custom answer correctness: 0.98 - The response is essentially correct and closely aligned with the expected answer.

**Duration**: 1m 10s

**Tokens**: in 276,195 out 1,212

````markdown
Investigation result: the payment failures are caused by a saturated PostgreSQL dependency in `shared-services`, not by the `payments-api` pod itself.

### Evidence

**Payment service state**
- Deployment `payments/payments-api` is healthy: `1/1` available.
- Pod `payments/payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc` is `Running`, `Ready`, `0` restarts.
- Active alert:
  - `PaymentErrorRateHigh` in `payments`
  - Description: `Payment error rate is 100.00%, which exceeds the 15% threshold.`

**Payment application logs**
`payments/payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc` is returning 503s because DB connections are rejected:
- `2026-09-28 20:55:58,465 ERROR Payment failed ... connection to server at "postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local" (172.30.185.0), port 5432 failed: FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`
- Repeats on every request.
- Requests immediately fail with:
  - `GET /api/v1/process-payment ... 503`

**Direct dependency from payments to shared-services**
`payments-api` only shows one external dependency in its env:
- `PGHOST=postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local`
- `PGPORT=5432`
- `PGDATABASE=demo`

So the failing cross-namespace dependency is:
- `shared-services/postgres` service at `172.30.185.0:5432`

### Root cause

PostgreSQL in `shared-services` has exhausted client connection capacity.

**Database evidence**
Current metrics from `shared-services/postgres`:
- `pg_settings_max_connections = 20`
- `pg_settings_superuser_reserved_connections = 3`
- `sum(pg_stat_activity_count{namespace="shared-services"}) = 18`
- `sum(pg_stat_activity_count{namespace="shared-services",state="idle in transaction"}) = 16`

This means almost all non-superuser slots are consumed, which matches the payment errors.

**Postgres logs**
`shared-services/postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj` shows continuous rejects:
- `2026-09-28 20:56:39.942 UTC ... FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`
- Same error repeats continuously through `20:57:27`.

### Likely source of the connection leak

`shared-services/reporting-service` is the strongest candidate.

**Why**
- It uses the same DB:
  - deployment `shared-services/reporting-service`
  - `PGHOST=postgres`
  - `PGPORT=5432`
- Its logs show a repeating pattern every 10s:
  - `INFO Open db connection`
  - `ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero`
- At `20:54:10` it starts failing with the same Postgres saturation error:
  - `connection to server at "postgres" (172.30.185.0), port 5432 failed: FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved...`

**Additional correlation**
- Current DB activity shows `16` sessions from user `dbuser` in `idle in transaction`.
- Both apps are using `PGUSER=dbuser`.

This strongly indicates `reporting-service` is opening DB sessions and not cleaning them up after its repeated `division by zero` failure, filling the connection pool until `payments-api` can no longer connect.

### Other dependencies checked

**`shared-services/reconciliation-service`**
- Pod: `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2`
- State: `CrashLoopBackOff`, `0/1`, 6 restarts
- Cause:
  - probes hit `http://:8443/`
  - container is `registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest`
  - events/logs show probe failures with `HTTP 400`
- This is unhealthy, but I found no evidence that `payments-api` depends on it. It is not the cause of the payment request failures.

**`shared-services/postgres` pod health**
- Pod `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj` is `2/2 Running`
- The DB process is up, but overloaded on connections.

### Conclusion

1. **Primary cause of customer request failures**
   - `payments/payments-api` cannot open DB connections to `shared-services/postgres`.
   - Exact error: `FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`.

2. **Most likely underlying cause**
   - `shared-services/reporting-service` is leaking DB connections after repeated `division by zero` errors.
   - Evidence: repeated `Open db connection` + failure loop, plus `16` `idle in transaction` sessions for `dbuser`.

3. **Other issue found**
   - `shared-services/reconciliation-service` is crash-looping due to bad probe configuration, but it does not appear causal for payments.

### Fix / mitigation

**Immediate mitigation**
- Stop the source of leaked DB connections:
  - scale down `shared-services/reporting-service` temporarily, or fix/redeploy it so connections are closed on error.
- That should free DB slots and allow `payments-api` to recover.

## Root cause: PostgreSQL connection exhaustion

`payments/payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc` is healthy at the Pod level but cannot open database connections. Its logs repeatedly show:

- `FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`
- Requests to `GET /api/v1/process-payment` return **503**
- The `PaymentErrorRateHigh` critical alert reports **100%** error rate.

The payment API is configured to use the cross-namespace dependency:

```text
postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local:5432 / database demo
```

The `shared-services/postgres` endpoint is reachable and its Pod is Ready, so this is **not** a DNS, Service, or network failure.

### Why PostgreSQL is exhausted

The database was explicitly configured with:

- `max_connections = 20`
- `superuser_reserved_connections = 3`

At investigation time, PostgreSQL had **16 `dbuser` connections** from `10.128.2.135`, which is the IP of:

```text
shared-services/reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg
```

All 16 are `idle in transaction`, beginning every 10 seconds from `20:51:40 UTC` through `20:55:30 UTC`. They are running:

```sql
SELECT count(*) FROM reports
```

`reporting-service` logs confirm the causal chain:

```text
INFO Open db connection
ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero
```

This repeats every 10 seconds. The service opens a database connection, encounters `division by zero`, and leaves the transaction/connection open instead of rolling it back and closing it. It then consumes the available non-superuser slots, preventing `payments-api` from connecting.

## Dependency status

| Dependency | Namespace | Status | Impact |
|---|---|---:|---|
| `postgres` | `shared-services` | Pod Ready; **connection pool exhausted** | **Direct cause of payment 503s** |
| `reporting-service` | `shared-services` | Pod Ready; application errors and connection leak | **Source of database exhaustion** |
| `reconciliation-service` | `shared-services` | `CrashLoopBackOff`, 0/1 ready | Separate failure; no evidence it causes the payment outage |

### Separate issue: reconciliation service

`shared-services/reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2` is crash-looping because both probes request **HTTP** on port **8443**:

```text
Liveness probe failed: HTTP probe failed with statuscode: 400
Readiness probe failed: HTTP probe failed with statuscode: 400
```

The container is `registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest`, which starts TLS on 8443; the probe configuration uses `scheme: HTTP` rather than HTTPS. This workload has no configured PostgreSQL environment variables, so it is not implicated in the payment connection exhaustion.

## Recommended mitigation and permanent fix

1. **Immediate, reversible mitigation:** stop or scale down `shared-services/reporting-service` to halt new leaked connections, then terminate its existing idle-in-transaction database sessions. This restores connection capacity for payments.
   - Restarting reporting alone will temporarily release its connections, but the leak will recur within minutes if it remains enabled.

2. **Permanent fix:** correct `reporting-service` so every exception path performs transaction rollback and closes/releases the database connection. The `division by zero` error must also be fixed or safely handled.

3. **Capacity guardrail:** keep `max_connections=20` only if it fits expected concurrency; otherwise raise it only after addressing the leak and setting bounded application connection pools. Raising the limit without fixing the leak delays recurrence rather than resolving it.

4. **Independent repair:** change the reconciliation liveness/readiness probe to HTTPS on port 8443, or point it to the application’s actual HTTP health endpoint/port.

**Evidence**
- `PaymentErrorRateHigh` is firing at **100%** error rate.
- `payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc` logs show every request failing with HTTP **503**, for example at **20:56:00 UTC**:
  > `FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`
- The payment deployment’s only configured external dependency is:
  - `postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local:5432`
  - database `demo`
- `shared-services/postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj` is Running, Ready, and has a valid Service endpoint (`10.128.2.133:5432`); this is **not** a DNS, network, or pod-availability failure.

## Dependency failure identified

PostgreSQL currently has `max_connections = 20`.

Current database session breakdown:
- **16** sessions owned by `dbuser`, all from `10.128.2.135` — `shared-services/reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg`
- All 16 are **`idle in transaction`**, indicating connections are being retained rather than released.
- PostgreSQL reserves a portion of slots for privileged users; normal clients such as payments are rejected once those are reached.

The database initialization ConfigMap, `shared-services/postgres-init`, explicitly sets:

```sql
ALTER SYSTEM SET max_connections = 20;
```

## Likely originating workload

`shared-services/reporting-service` version `quay.io/afalossi/ts01-reporting-service:v1.0.2` is leaking the database connections.

**Evidence**
- Its logs repeatedly show:
  - `Open db connection`
  - `ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero`
- This repeats every 10 seconds from **20:51:40 UTC** through **20:54:00 UTC**, followed by database slot-exhaustion failures beginning at **20:54:10 UTC**.
- The `dbuser` sessions on PostgreSQL all originate from the reporting pod IP.
- Payments failures began at **20:56 UTC**, after the pool had already been exhausted.

## Other cross-namespace dependency findings

1. **PostgreSQL — impaired / direct cause**
   - Namespace: `shared-services`
   - Workload: `postgres`
   - Service endpoint exists and database is running, but it is unable to admit normal client connections.

2. **Reporting service — unhealthy application behavior / source of exhaustion**
   - Namespace: `shared-services`
   - Pod: `reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg`
   - It is Ready but functionally unhealthy: its recurring `division by zero` error is associated with 16 leaked `idle in transaction` PostgreSQL sessions.

3. **Reconciliation service — separate issue, not evidenced as a payment dependency**
   - Namespace: `shared-services`
   - Pod: `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2`
   - Status: `CrashLoopBackOff`, no Service endpoints.
   - Cause: its probes request HTTP on port `8443`, while the httpd container returns HTTP **400**. Events show both readiness and liveness probe failures.
   - `payments-api` has no reconciliation endpoint configured, so this does not explain the customer payment failures.

## Recommended mitigation

1. **Immediate, reversible:** scale `shared-services/reporting-service` to zero to terminate its leaked sessions and stop new leaks. This disables reporting processing temporarily but should allow payment requests to reconnect once PostgreSQL frees those sessions.
2. **Correct the reporting-service defect:** fix the division-by-zero path and ensure database connections/transactions are closed or rolled back on every error path. Then redeploy it.
3. **Capacity safeguard:** reassess the PostgreSQL limit of 20 connections. Increasing it can reduce immediate sensitivity, but it is not a replacement for fixing the leak. A PostgreSQL restart is required for a `max_connections` change to take effect.
4. **Separately fix reconciliation probes:** align the liveness/readiness probe scheme and port with the actual service listener.
````

````markdown
### Root Cause Analysis

The `payments-api` service in the `payments` namespace is failing 100% of customer requests (returning HTTP 503) because it cannot establish a connection to its backend PostgreSQL database in the `shared-services` namespace. 

The PostgreSQL connection pool is completely exhausted (`FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`) due to a connection leak caused by **`reporting-service`** in the `shared-services` namespace repeatedly opening database connections without closing them upon encountering an unhandled error.

---

### Investigation Findings & Evidence

#### 1. `payments` Namespace
* **Workload**: `Deployment/payments-api` (Pod: `payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc`)
* **Alert**: `PaymentErrorRateHigh` (Critical — 100.00% payment failure rate).
* **Logs**:
  ```text
  ERROR Payment failed tx=767714 amount=450.56 TWD: connection to server at "postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local" (172.30.185.0), port 5432 failed: FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute
  INFO 127.0.0.1:51972 - "GET /api/v1/process-payment HTTP/1.1" 503
  ```

---

#### 2. `shared-services` Namespace (Dependencies)

##### A. `postgres` (Service & Deployment)
* **Pod**: `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj`
* **Alerts**: `PostgresqlTooManyConnections` (Critical) and `PostgresqlConnectionsHigh` (Warning).
* **Metrics (`pg_stat_activity_count`)**:
  * 16 connections held in `idle in transaction` state by user `dbuser`.
  * Total connections reached the maximum non-superuser threshold.
* **Logs**:
  ```text
  FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute
  ```

##### B. `reporting-service` (Deployment & Pod)
* **Pod**: `reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg` (Image: `quay.io/afalossi/ts01-reporting-service:v1.0.2`)
* **Root Cause of DB Exhaustion**: The service attempts a job every 10 seconds, hits an unhandled `division by zero` exception, and leaks the database connection in `idle in transaction` state.
* **Logs**:
  ```text
  INFO Open db connection
  ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero
  ```

##### C. `reconciliation-service` (Deployment & Pod)
* **Pod**: `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2`
* **Status**: `CrashLoopBackOff` (0/1 Ready)
* **Alert**: `SharedServicesPodCrashLooping`
* **Cause**: Liveness and readiness probes are sending plaintext `HTTP` requests to port `8443` (an SSL/TLS HTTPS endpoint), returning `HTTP 400 Bad Request` and repeatedly killing the container:
  ```text
  Warning  Unhealthy  Liveness probe failed: HTTP probe failed with statuscode: 400
  Warning  Unhealthy  Readiness probe failed: HTTP probe failed with statuscode: 400
  ```

---

### Remediation Steps

1. **Mitigate Database Connection Exhaustion**:
   * Scale down or restart `reporting-service` to immediately terminate the orphaned connections:
     ```bash
     oc scale deployment/reporting-service -n shared-services --replicas=0
     ```
   * Alternatively, restart the `postgres` pod in `shared-services` or terminate idle sessions via SQL (`pg_terminate_backend`).
   * Fix the `division by zero` logic and ensure connection pooling/closing with proper error-handling (`try...finally`) in `reporting-service`.

2. **Fix `reconciliation-service` Probes**:
   * Update the deployment probe configuration in `shared-services` to target port `8080` (HTTP) instead of `8443`, or set `scheme: HTTPS` if probing port `8443`.
````

     INFO 127.0.0.1:... - "GET /api/v1/process-payment HTTP/1.1" 503
     ```
   - Alert firing: `PaymentErrorRateHigh` (100% payment failure rate).

2. **Database Connection Pool Exhaustion (`shared-services` namespace):**
   - PostgreSQL configuration: `max_connections = 20`, `superuser_reserved_connections = 3` (leaving 17 slots for standard users).
   - Metric `pg_stat_database_numbackends{datname="demo"}` shows 18 active connections.

   - Pod `reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg` logs repeated unhandled exceptions every 10 seconds:
     ```text
     INFO Open db connection
     ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero
     ```
   - The failure path in `reporting-service` leaks open PostgreSQL connections on every iteration until all connection slots are consumed.

4. **Additional Dependency Issue Observed (`shared-services` namespace):**
   - Pod `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2` is in `CrashLoopBackOff`.
   - **Cause:** Probes are configured for plain HTTP (`scheme: HTTP`) against port `8443`, but Apache is listening with SSL enabled, resulting in `HTTP 400 Bad Request` probe failures.

---

### Root Causes

1. **Primary Root Cause:** A bug in `reporting-service` (v1.0.2) causes a runtime division-by-zero error, leaking PostgreSQL connections every 10 seconds until `max_connections` (20) on `postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local` is exhausted.
2. **Cascading Failure:** `payments-api` is unable to acquire a connection to the PostgreSQL instance in `shared-services`, rejecting all incoming customer transactions.

### Recommended Remediation Steps

1. **Immediate Mitigation (Restore Payment Processing):**
   - Roll back or scale down `reporting-service` in the `shared-services` namespace to stop connection leaks:
     ```bash

     # Or rollback to previous revision v1.0.1:
     oc rollout undo deployment/reporting-service -n shared-services
     ```
   - Restart the PostgreSQL pod `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj` (or terminate leaked idle connections in PostgreSQL) to clear exhausted connection slots.
   - Verify `payments-api` recovers and processes requests.

2. **Fix `reporting-service`:**
   - Resolve the division-by-zero calculation in `reporting-service` and ensure database connections are closed/released in error-handling `finally` blocks or managed connection pools.

3. **Fix `reconciliation-service` Probes:**
   - Update `reconciliation-service` deployment probes to use HTTPS (`scheme: HTTPS`) on port `8443` or target the plaintext HTTP port `8080`.

2. **Clear leaked PostgreSQL connections:**
   Restart the `postgres` pod or terminate the leaked backend connections to free up slots immediately:
   ```bash
   oc rollout restart deployment/postgres -n shared-services
   ```
3. **Fix probe configuration for `reconciliation-service`:**
   Update the probe scheme to `HTTPS` or point to the correct HTTP port (`8080`) on `reconciliation-service`.

````markdown
### Summary
The payment service (`payments-api`) in the **`payments`** namespace is failing 100% of customer requests (HTTP 503) due to connection exhaustion on its backend PostgreSQL database located in the **`shared-services`** namespace. 

The exhaustion is caused by a connection leak bug introduced in the newly deployed version (`v1.0.2`) of **`reporting-service`** in `shared-services`.

---

### Evidence & Findings

#### 1. Affected Service (`payments` namespace)
* **Workload:** Deployment `payments-api` / Pod `payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc`
* **Active Alerts:**
  * `PaymentErrorRateHigh` (Critical): Error rate is 100.00% (exceeding 15% threshold).
* **Observed Errors & Metrics:**
  * Pod logs show repeated 503 errors on `GET /api/v1/process-payment`:
    ```
    ERROR Payment failed tx=...: connection to server at "postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local" (172.30.185.0), port 5432 failed: FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute
    INFO 127.0.0.1:... - "GET /api/v1/process-payment HTTP/1.1" 503
    ```
  * Metrics (`http_requests_total{namespace="payments"}`): 130 failed requests (code 503) vs 105 successful (code 200).

#### 2. Cross-Namespace Dependencies (`shared-services` namespace)
`payments-api` depends directly on `postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local:5432` (database `demo`, role `dbuser`).

* **Database Workload:** Deployment `postgres` / Pod `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj`
  * Configured `max_connections`: **20** (via `postgres-init` ConfigMap). Non-superuser connection slots are capped at 17.
  * Active Alerts: `PostgresqlTooManyConnections` (Critical) and `PostgresqlConnectionsHigh` (Warning).
  * Current metric: `global_connections_connections_total = 18` (exhausting all non-superuser slots).

* **Source of the Leak:** Deployment `reporting-service` / Pod `reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg`
  * Image: `quay.io/afalossi/ts01-reporting-service:v1.0.2` (recently deployed, replacing `v1.0.1`).
  * Inspection of `pg_stat_activity` on `postgres` revealed 15 connections in `idle in transaction` status originating from `10.128.2.135` (`reporting-service` pod IP):
    ```sql
    dbuser | 10.128.2.135 | idle in transaction | SELECT count(*) FROM reports
    ```
  * In `reporting-service` (`app.py`), the loop runs every 10 seconds, opens a new connection (`conn = psycopg2.connect()`), appends it to a global list `connections.append(conn)`, and hits a division by zero error (`x = total / 0`). Because exception handling never closes the connection, it leaks one connection every 10 seconds until all 20 connection slots are consumed.

#### 3. Additional Dependency Finding
* **Workload:** Deployment `reconciliation-service` / Pod `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2`
  * Status: `CrashLoopBackOff` (Alert: `SharedServicesPodCrashLooping`).
  * Cause: The liveness/readiness probes send an unencrypted `HTTP` GET to SSL port `8443` (`scheme: HTTP`, `port: 8443`), which Apache rejects with HTTP 400 Bad Request, killing the container repeatedly.

---

### Root Cause
1. **Primary Root Cause:** A code bug in `reporting-service:v1.0.2` in `shared-services` leaks unclosed PostgreSQL connections on every loop iteration due to an unhandled `ZeroDivisionError`.
2. **Cascading Failure:** With PostgreSQL's `max_connections` set to 20, all non-superuser connection slots were quickly exhausted, blocking `payments-api` from opening connections to process payment requests.

---

### Recommended Remediation

#### Immediate Mitigations
1. **Scale down or roll back `reporting-service`** *(Reversible)*:
   Roll back to `v1.0.1` or scale `reporting-service` to 0 replicas to release all leaked connections immediately:
   ```bash
   oc rollout undo deployment/reporting-service -n shared-services
   # or
   oc scale deployment/reporting-service --replicas=0 -n shared-services
   ```
   *As soon as the pod terminates, its open connection handles will close, allowing `payments-api` to immediately resume successful operations.*

2. **Terminate idle connections in PostgreSQL** *(Reversible)*:
   If connections persist before pod cleanup, terminate the idle backends in PostgreSQL:
   ```sql
   SELECT pg_terminate_backend(pid) FROM pg_stat_activity WHERE usename = 'dbuser' AND state = 'idle in transaction';
   ```

#### Permanent Fixes
1. **Fix `reporting-service` code:**
   * Remove the `x = total / 0` division error.
   * Ensure database connections are always closed using a context manager (`with conn:`) or a `finally: conn.close()` block.
2. **Postgres Connection Resiliency:**
   * Implement connection pooling (such as PgBouncer) or raise `max_connections` in `postgres-init` ConfigMap to prevent individual client leaks from exhausting global database capacity.
3. **Fix `reconciliation-service` probes:**
   * Update the deployment liveness/readiness probes to either use `scheme: HTTPS` on port 8443 or target the HTTP port `8080`.
````

````markdown
### Diagnostic Summary

The **`payments-api`** service in the **`payments`** namespace is failing all incoming payment requests with **HTTP 503 (Database unavailable)** due to connection exhaustion on its backend PostgreSQL database located in the **`shared-services`** namespace. 

The connection exhaustion is caused by a connection leak bug introduced in a recent update of **`reporting-service`** in the **`shared-services`** namespace. Additionally, another dependency in `shared-services`, **`reconciliation-service`**, is failing in **`CrashLoopBackOff`** due to a misconfigured health probe.

---

### Evidence

#### 1. Payment Service (`payments` namespace)
* **Resource**: Deployment `payments-api`, Pod `payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc`
* **Alert**: `PaymentErrorRateHigh` (Critical, firing at 100% error rate)
* **Pod Logs**:
  ```text
  ERROR Payment failed tx=683732 amount=343.82 CHF: connection to server at "postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local" (172.30.185.0), port 5432 failed: FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute
  INFO 127.0.0.1:54466 - "GET /api/v1/process-payment HTTP/1.1" 503
  ```
* **Traffic Flow**: `payments-api` routes requests through `/api/v1/process-payment`, which opens a PostgreSQL connection to `postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local:5432`.

---

#### 2. Dependencies in the `shared-services` Namespace

##### Dependency A: `postgres`
* **Resource**: Deployment `postgres`, Pod `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj`, Service `postgres:5432`
* **Alerts**: `PostgresqlTooManyConnections` (Critical), `PostgresqlConnectionsHigh` (Warning)
* **Configuration**: `postgres-init` ConfigMap sets `max_connections = 20`. PostgreSQL reserves 3 connections for superuser maintenance (`superuser_reserved_connections = 3`), allowing a maximum of 17 non-superuser connections.
* **Metrics & Status**:
  * Instant PromQL query `pg_stat_activity_count{namespace="shared-services"}` confirms **16 connections** in state `idle in transaction` under user `dbuser`, and 1 connection under `demo`.
  * Instant PromQL query `reporting_active_connections{namespace="shared-services"}` reports exactly **16 open connections** held by `reporting-service`.
  * All available non-superuser connection slots (17/17) are saturated.

##### Dependency B: `reporting-service` (Source of Database Connection Exhaustion)
* **Resource**: Deployment `reporting-service`, Pod `reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg` (Image: `quay.io/afalossi/ts01-reporting-service:v1.0.2`, recently rolled out at revision 2)
* **Pod Logs**:
  ```text
  INFO Starting reporting-service v1.0.2
  INFO Open db connection
  ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero
  INFO Open db connection
  ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero
  ```
* **Application Code Analysis (`/app/app.py`)**:
  ```python
  with query_duration.time():
      conn = psycopg2.connect()
      connection_failures = 0
      active_connections.inc()
      connections.append(conn)
      cur = conn.cursor()
      cur.execute("SELECT count(*) FROM reports")
      total = cur.fetchone()[0]
      x = total / 0 # <------------ BUG
  ```
  Every 10 seconds, `reporting-service` establishes a new DB connection and appends it to a list. It then triggers a `ZeroDivisionError` (`total / 0`), which jumps to the exception handler without closing the connection. Over time, it exhausted all non-superuser connection slots in PostgreSQL.

##### Dependency C: `reconciliation-service` (CrashLoopBackOff)
* **Resource**: Deployment `reconciliation-service`, Pod `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2`
* **Alerts**: `SharedServicesPodCrashLooping` (Warning), `SharedServicesDeploymentReplicasMismatch` (Warning)
* **Pod State**: `0/1` Ready, `CrashLoopBackOff` (Restart count: 6+)
* **Failure Cause**:
  * Pod runs Apache HTTPD (`registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest`) with TLS/SSL configured on port `8443`.
  * The liveness/readiness probes in the Deployment spec are configured with plain HTTP:
    ```yaml
    httpGet:
      path: /
      port: 8443
      scheme: HTTP
    ```
  * Apache logs: `"GET / HTTP/1.0" 400 362` (HTTP 400 Bad Request because plain HTTP is sent to an SSL port).
  * The probes fail 3 consecutive times, causing the kubelet to send `SIGTERM` and restart the container continuously.

---

### Root Causes

1. **Payment Failures**: `payments-api` cannot connect to PostgreSQL because `reporting-service` in `shared-services` leaked all connection slots due to an unhandled `division by zero` bug in `v1.0.2`.
2. **Reconciliation Service Crashes**: `reconciliation-service` container in `shared-services` is killed by the kubelet liveness probe because it sends plain `HTTP` requests to an SSL/TLS-enabled port (`8443`).

---

### Recommended Remediation

#### Immediate Fix for `payments-api` & `postgres`
1. **Roll back or scale down `reporting-service`**:
   * Roll back `reporting-service` to version `v1.0.1`:
     ```sh
     oc rollout undo deployment/reporting-service -n shared-services
     ```
     *(Or scale down `reporting-service` to 0 replicas temporarily to release all held connections).*
2. **Terminate leaked connections on PostgreSQL**:
   * Restarting the `postgres` pod or terminating idle connections will immediately restore database access for `payments-api`:
     ```sh
     oc delete pod -l app=postgres -n shared-services
     ```
3. **Tune PostgreSQL max connections (Optional)**:
   * Update the `postgres-init` ConfigMap in `shared-services` to set a higher `max_connections` value (e.g., 100) and deploy a connection pooler (e.g., PgBouncer) to prevent single-client exhaustion.

#### Fix for `reconciliation-service`
* Update the liveness and readiness probe schemes in `deployment/reconciliation-service` to `HTTPS` (or target the HTTP port `8080` instead of `8443`):
  ```yaml
  livenessProbe:
    httpGet:
      path: /
      port: 8443
      scheme: HTTPS
  readinessProbe:
    httpGet:
      path: /
      port: 8443
      scheme: HTTPS
  ```
````

   * **Alert:** `PaymentErrorRateHigh` (Critical — 100% error rate).
   * **Pod Logs:** Inbound requests to `GET /api/v1/process-payment` fail with HTTP 503:
     ```text
     ERROR Payment failed tx=... : connection to server at "postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local" (172.30.185.0), port 5432 failed: FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute
     INFO 127.0.0.1:... - "GET /api/v1/process-payment HTTP/1.1" 503
     ```

2. **Database Dependency (`shared-services` namespace):**
   * **Pod:** `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj` (Service `postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local`)
   * **Configuration:** `max_connections` is set to `20` (in ConfigMap `postgres-init`), with `superuser_reserved_connections` set to `3`, allowing only 17 non-superuser connections.
   * **Alerts:** `PostgresqlConnectionsHigh` and `PostgresqlTooManyConnections` (Critical).
   * **Connection Inspection (`pg_stat_activity`):** 18 connections are active on database `demo`. 15 of these connections are held in state `idle in transaction` by client IP `10.128.2.135` executing `SELECT count(*) FROM reports`.

3. **Inter-namespace Workload Dependencies (`shared-services` namespace):**
   * **Pod `reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg` (IP `10.128.2.135`):**
     * Recently updated to image `quay.io/afalossi/ts01-reporting-service:v1.0.2`.
     * Logs show recurring errors every 10 seconds:
       ```text
       INFO Open db connection
       ERROR Failed to process pending reports: division by zero
       ```
     * Application code (`/app/app.py`) reveals a connection leak: every 10 seconds it runs `connections.append(conn)` followed by `x = total / 0 # <------------ BUG`. The unhandled exception aborts execution before the connection can be closed, leaving the connection open in `idle in transaction` until the PostgreSQL pool is exhausted.
   * **Pod `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2`:**
     * Status: `CrashLoopBackOff` (6 restarts).
     * Alerts: `SharedServicesPodCrashLooping` and `SharedServicesDeploymentReplicasMismatch`.
     * Logs show Apache `httpd` receives plain HTTP probe requests on its TLS port:
       ```text
       [ssl:warn] AH01909: 10.128.2.132:8443:0 server certificate does NOT include an ID which matches the server name
       "GET / HTTP/1.0" 400 362
       ```
     * The liveness/readiness probes are configured with `scheme: HTTP` against port `8443`, triggering HTTP 400 Bad Request responses and container restarts. (Note: `payments-api` does not call this service directly, but it is failing within the same application group).

---

### Root Cause

* **Primary Cause:** Customer payment requests fail because PostgreSQL in `shared-services` has exhausted its available client connection pool (`max_connections = 20`).
* **Trigger:** `reporting-service` v1.0.2 introduced a division-by-zero error on pending report processing. Because connections are appended to an in-memory list without cleanup on failure, it leaked 15 database connections within minutes, locking out `payments-api` from connecting to the database.

---

### Remediation

1. **Mitigate the Connection Leak in `reporting-service`:**
   * Roll back `reporting-service` to `v1.0.1`:
     ```bash
     oc rollout undo deployment/reporting-service -n shared-services
     ```
     *(Alternatively, scale down the deployment temporarily: `oc scale deployment/reporting-service --replicas=0 -n shared-services`)*.
   * Fix `/app/app.py` in `reporting-service` to remove the division-by-zero bug and manage database connections in a `try...finally` or context manager block (`with conn:`, `conn.close()`).

2. **Clear Leaked PostgreSQL Connections:**
   * Restart the PostgreSQL pod to clear hung connections:
     ```bash
     oc delete pod postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj -n shared-services
     ```
   * Or terminate the idle backends directly via `psql`:
     ```sql
     SELECT pg_terminate_backend(pid) FROM pg_stat_activity WHERE usename = 'dbuser' AND state = 'idle in transaction';
     ```

3. **Fix `reconciliation-service` Health Probes:**
   * Update the deployment's probes to use `scheme: HTTPS` on port 8443, or point HTTP probes to port 8080:
     ```bash
     oc patch deployment reconciliation-service -n shared-services --type='json' -p='[
       {"op": "replace", "path": "/spec/template/spec/containers/0/livenessProbe/httpGet/scheme", "value": "HTTPS"},
       {"op": "replace", "path": "/spec/template/spec/containers/0/readinessProbe/httpGet/scheme", "value": "HTTPS"}
     ]'
     ```

The **payment service is experiencing a 100% error rate** because the shared PostgreSQL database in the `shared-services` namespace has exhausted all available connections. Here's the full causality chain:

### 1. Root Cause: Connection Leak in `reporting-service` (namespace: `shared-services`)

- **Wrong image**: Uses `registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest` (a generic Apache HTTPD image), not a reconciliation service application
- **Broken health probes**: Liveness/readiness probes send `HTTP GET /` to port `8443` using `scheme: HTTP`, but the HTTPD container serves HTTPS on port 8443 → returns **400 Bad Request** → liveness probe fails → container gets killed repeatedly

---

### Immediate — Restore payment service (reversible):

1. **Kill the leaked connections** by restarting the `reporting-service` pod to release the 16 idle-in-transaction connections:

2. **Scale down `reporting-service`** to stop the leak until the bug is fixed:
   ```
   oc scale deployment reporting-service -n shared-services --replicas=0
   ```

### Short-term — Fix the bugs:

3. **Roll back `reporting-service`** to `v1.0.1` (the previous version before the `v1.0.2` update that introduced the connection leak and `division by zero` bug):
   ```
   oc rollout undo deployment/reporting-service -n shared-services
   ```

4. **Increase `max_connections`** on PostgreSQL as a safety margin (e.g., to 100) to prevent a single misbehaving client from taking down all services.

### Fix `reconciliation-service`:

5. Deploy the correct application image (not `httpd-24`) and fix the health probe configuration to use `scheme: HTTPS` on port 8443, or point probes to the correct application health endpoint.

## Diagnosis: Payment Service Failure

### Root Cause: PostgreSQL Connection Exhaustion in `shared-services` Namespace

The payment service (`payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc` in namespace `payments`) is returning **HTTP 503 on 100% of requests** because it cannot connect to its PostgreSQL database. The database is hosted in the `shared-services` namespace and has **exhausted all available connection slots**.

### Evidence Chain

#### 1. PostgreSQL Connection Pool Saturated
- **Database pod**: `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj` in `shared-services`
- **`max_connections` = 20** (very low)
- **`superuser_reserved_connections` = 3** → only **17 slots** available for regular users
- **Current connections**: **16 connections in `idle in transaction` state** held by user `dbuser`, plus 1 active and 1 idle by user `demo` = **18 total** — all 17 non-superuser slots consumed
- PostgreSQL is rejecting every new connection with: `FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`

#### 2. Connection Leak by `reporting-service` (Primary Culprit)
The `reporting-service-6d7f67656f-2ffhg` in `shared-services` was **recently updated from v1.0.1 → v1.0.2** (deployment revision 2). Since the update:
- It opens a new DB connection **every 10 seconds** (`Open db connection`)
- Every attempt fails with `division by zero`, but the connections are **never closed** — they remain in `idle in transaction` state
- **16 leaked connections** by user `dbuser` are visible in `pg_stat_activity`, all stuck in `idle in transaction`
- The bug was introduced in the **v1.0.2 image update** — the previous v1.0.1 was replaced at ~20:51:39

#### 3. Cascading Failure to Payment Service
- `payments-api` in namespace `payments` connects to `postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local:5432` using user credentials from secret `payments-db`
- With all connection slots consumed by the reporting service leak, every payment transaction fails with 503
- **Alert `PaymentErrorRateHigh` (critical)**: 100% error rate, exceeding the 15% threshold

#### 4. Additional Issue: `reconciliation-service` CrashLoopBackOff
- Pod `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2` in `shared-services` is in **CrashLoopBackOff** (6 restarts)
- **Wrong container image**: deployed with `registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest` (a generic Apache HTTPD image), not an actual reconciliation service application
- **Liveness/readiness probes misconfigured**: probing port `8443` with `scheme: HTTP` — the HTTPD container listens on 8443 with HTTPS (TLS), so HTTP probes get `400 Bad Request`, causing repeated kills
- This is a separate deployment issue, not directly causing the connection leak, but it means the reconciliation service is also non-functional

### Affected Components Summary

| Component | Namespace | Status | Issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| `payments-api` | `payments` | Running but 100% errors | Cannot connect to DB |
| `postgres` | `shared-services` | Running but saturated | 18/20 connections used, 16 leaked |
| `reporting-service` | `shared-services` | Running but leaking | v1.0.2 bug: division by zero + connection leak |
| `reconciliation-service` | `shared-services` | CrashLoopBackOff | Wrong image + probe misconfiguration |

### Recommended Fix

**Immediate mitigation (restore payment service):**

1. **Roll back `reporting-service` to v1.0.1** to stop the connection leak:
   ```
   oc -n shared-services set image deployment/reporting-service reporting-service=quay.io/afalossi/ts01-reporting-service:v1.0.1
   ```
   This will terminate the leaking pod and release the 16 stuck connections. *(Reversible — can re-deploy v1.0.2 after the bug is fixed.)*

2. If connections remain stuck after rollback, **restart the postgres pod** to force-close all connections:
   ```
   oc -n shared-services delete pod postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj
   ```
   *(The Deployment will recreate it. Note: brief downtime for all DB-dependent services.)*

**Longer-term fixes:**

3. **Increase `max_connections`** on the PostgreSQL instance from 20 to a more appropriate value (e.g., 100+) to provide headroom.
4. **Fix the `reconciliation-service` deployment**: replace the `registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest` image with the correct application image, and fix the probes to use `scheme: HTTPS` on port 8443 (or probe the correct application port).
5. **Fix the v1.0.2 reporting-service bug**: the `division by zero` error and connection leak need to be resolved in the application code before redeploying.

Let me dig deeper into the payment service logs and investigate the dependencies in the `shared-services` namespace, which has multiple alerts firing.The picture is becoming clear. The payment service is failing because PostgreSQL has exhausted its connection slots. Let me now investigate the crash-looping reconciliation service and check the PostgreSQL connection metrics to understand what's consuming all the connections.Now let me check the PostgreSQL connection metrics and the reporting service's connection leak behavior.Now let me check the reporting service deployment to see if it was recently updated (it went from v1.0.1 to v1.0.2), which may have introduced the connection leak.I now have a complete picture. Here's the full diagnosis:

---

## Root Cause Analysis: Payment Service Failure

### Summary
**100% of payment requests are failing** (HTTP 503) because the PostgreSQL database in the `shared-services` namespace has exhausted all available connection slots. This is caused by a **connection leak in the `reporting-service`** introduced by a recent image update.

---

### Causality Chain

#### 1. 🔴 Root Cause: `reporting-service` Connection Leak (namespace: `shared-services`)

The `reporting-service` deployment was updated from **v1.0.1 → v1.0.2** (revision 2). The new version has a bug — it opens a new database connection every 10 seconds (`"Open db connection"`) but **never closes them**, and each connection hits a `division by zero` error leaving the transaction uncommitted.

**Evidence:**
- Logs show repeated `Open db connection` followed by `Failed to process pending reports: division by zero` every ~10 seconds
- PostgreSQL metrics confirm **16 connections in `idle in transaction` state** from user `dbuser` on the `demo` database — these are leaked connections that were never committed or rolled back
- The connections grew from **4 → 16** over the last ~10 minutes
- The `reporting-service` connects using credentials from secret `reporting-db` (user: `dbuser`)

#### 2. 🔴 PostgreSQL Connection Exhaustion (namespace: `shared-services`)

PostgreSQL pod `postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj` is configured with:
- **`max_connections` = 20**
- **`superuser_reserved_connections` = 3**
- **Usable connections for regular users = 17**
- **Currently used: 18 backends** on the `demo` database (16 idle-in-transaction + 1 active + 1 idle)

All non-superuser connection slots are consumed. PostgreSQL rejects every new connection with:
> `FATAL: remaining connection slots are reserved for roles with the SUPERUSER attribute`

**Firing alerts:**
- `PostgresqlTooManyConnections` (critical) — 18 active connections
- `PostgresqlConnectionsHigh` (warning) — 18 connections

#### 3. 🔴 Payment Service Impact (namespace: `payments`)

Pod `payments-api-559656657c-2zwpc` (image: `ts01-payments-api:v1.0.1`) connects to `postgres.shared-services.svc.cluster.local:5432` to process payments. Since all connection slots are exhausted, **every payment transaction fails** with HTTP 503.

**Firing alerts:**
- `PaymentErrorRateHigh` (critical) — error rate at **100%**, threshold 15%

---

### Additional Issue: `reconciliation-service` CrashLoopBackOff (namespace: `shared-services`)

Pod `reconciliation-service-587768b96f-9r9f2` is in **CrashLoopBackOff** (6 restarts). This is a **separate issue** — the deployment uses the wrong container image (`registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest`, a generic Apache HTTPD image) instead of an actual reconciliation service. The health probes (liveness/readiness) target port `8443` with HTTP scheme, but the HTTPD container serves HTTPS on that port, causing HTTP 400 responses and repeated liveness probe failures.

---

### Recommended Fix

**Immediate mitigation (reversible):**

1. **Stop the connection leak** — Scale down the `reporting-service` to 0 replicas to stop new leaked connections:
   ```
   oc scale deployment/reporting-service -n shared-services --replicas=0
   ```

2. **Kill idle-in-transaction connections** — The leaked connections won't release on their own. Restart the PostgreSQL pod to clear them:
   ```
   oc delete pod postgres-5bd4d575fb-ghgqj -n shared-services
   ```
   Or, if you have superuser access, connect to PostgreSQL and terminate the idle sessions with `SELECT pg_terminate_backend(pid)`.

3. **Verify recovery** — After connections are freed, the `payments-api` should immediately resume processing payments.

**Permanent fixes:**

4. **Roll back `reporting-service`** to the working version v1.0.1 (reversible):
   ```
   oc set image deployment/reporting-service reporting-service=quay.io/afalossi/ts01-reporting-service:v1.0.1 -n shared-services
   ```

5. **Fix the `reconciliation-service`** deployment — replace the incorrect `registry.redhat.io/rhel9/httpd-24:latest` image with the actual reconciliation service image, and fix the health probe configuration (use HTTPS scheme for port 8443, or target the correct application port).

6. **Increase `max_connections`** on PostgreSQL (e.g., to 100+) and/or configure `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` to automatically kill leaked connections in the future.
````

I investigated `memcached` in namespace `cache-tier`.

## Diagnosis

### Evidence
1. **Pod is not running**
   - Pod: `cache-tier/memcached-6b9c6599f-zbx7f`
   - Status: `Pending`
   - Ready: `0/1`

2. **Scheduler is blocked by an unbound PVC**
   - Event on pod `memcached-6b9c6599f-zbx7f`:
     - `FailedScheduling`
     - Message: `0/6 nodes are available: pod has unbound immediate PersistentVolumeClaims`

3. **The PVC cannot be provisioned because the requested StorageClass does not exist**
   - PVC: `cache-tier/memcached-data-pvc`
   - Phase: `Pending`
   - Event on PVC:
     - `ProvisioningFailed`
     - Message: `storageclass.storage.k8s.io "standard-v2" not found`

4. **Cluster alert confirms the same issue**
   - Alert: `CacheTierPersistentVolumeClaimPending`
   - Resource: `cache-tier/memcached-data-pvc`
   - Summary: `PVC memcached-data-pvc is stuck in Pending`

5. **The service has no endpoints**
   - Service: `cache-tier/memcached`
   - `Endpoints` object exists but has no addresses
   - `EndpointSlice` `memcached-xzkjt` has `ENDPOINTS <unset>`
   - This is because no memcached pod has started successfully

### Root cause
`memcached` is unavailable because its pod depends on PVC `memcached-data-pvc`, and that PVC requests **StorageClass `standard-v2`**, which does not exist in the cluster.

Configured on PVC:
- `storageClassName: standard-v2`

Available StorageClasses in the cluster:
- `gp2-csi`
- `gp3-csi` **(default)**

Because the PVC never binds, the pod never schedules, so the service never gets endpoints.

## Fix / mitigation

### Recommended fix
Update or recreate `cache-tier/memcached-data-pvc` to use a valid StorageClass, preferably:
- `gp3-csi` (default), or
- `gp2-csi` if specifically required

### Important note
`spec.storageClassName` on an existing PVC is effectively not safely changeable for this use case. The usual fix is:

1. Delete `Deployment` `cache-tier/memcached`
2. Delete PVC `cache-tier/memcached-data-pvc`
3. Recreate the PVC with a valid StorageClass such as `gp3-csi`
4. Recreate/apply the deployment

### Example target correction
PVC should use:
- `storageClassName: gp3-csi`

## Additional note
I did not find evidence yet of a second blocker preventing startup. However, after fixing storage, the pod should still be watched because the deployment includes an init container:
- `init-perms` running `busybox`
- Security context has `runAsNonRoot: true`

That may or may not be compatible with the image/command at runtime, but it is **not** the current cause of unavailability. The current hard blocker is the missing StorageClass.

## Summary
**Primary cause:** invalid PVC StorageClass  
- Affected deployment: `cache-tier/memcached`
- Affected pod: `cache-tier/memcached-6b9c6599f-zbx7f`
- Affected PVC: `cache-tier/memcached-data-pvc`
- Exact error: `storageclass.storage.k8s.io "standard-v2" not found`

If you want, I can also prepare the corrected PVC/deployment YAML for `cache-tier`.
````

## Diagnosis: memcached is unavailable because its PVC cannot be provisioned

### Evidence
- Deployment `cache-tier/memcached` is **0/1 available**.
- Its only pod, `cache-tier/memcached-6b9c6599f-zbx7f`, is **Pending** and has never been scheduled.
- Scheduler event:
  > `0/6 nodes are available: pod has unbound immediate PersistentVolumeClaims.`
- The required PVC, `cache-tier/memcached-data-pvc`, is **Pending** and requests `2Gi` using StorageClass `standard-v2`.
- PVC provisioning event:
  > `storageclass.storage.k8s.io "standard-v2" not found`
- The cluster provides only:
  - `gp2-csi`
  - `gp3-csi` (**default**)
- The `cache-tier/memcached` Service has **no Endpoints**, since there is no ready backing pod.

### Root cause
The Memcached deployment references PVC `memcached-data-pvc`, but that claim specifies a nonexistent StorageClass: `standard-v2`. The unbound claim prevents pod scheduling, so Memcached never starts.

### Fix
Update the PVC/workload manifest to use an existing StorageClass—normally `gp3-csi`—and recreate the pending PVC. `storageClassName` is immutable after PVC creation, so the existing claim must be deleted and recreated.

Because the pod has not started and the PVC is unbound, deleting this PVC is **reversible and does not delete application data**.

Use a PVC definition equivalent to:

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: PersistentVolumeClaim
metadata:
  name: memcached-data-pvc
  namespace: cache-tier
  labels:
    app: memcached
spec:
  accessModes:
    - ReadWriteOnce
  storageClassName: gp3-csi
  resources:
    requests:
      storage: 2Gi
```

Alternatively, if persistence is unnecessary—as is typical for Memcached—remove the PVC volume, mount, and `init-perms` init container from the Deployment. This avoids storage provisioning entirely.

### Additional configuration risk
After storage is fixed, the init container executes `chmod 777 /cache` while configured as non-root under the `restricted-v2` SCC. It may fail with a permissions error depending on the provisioned volume’s ownership and mount behavior. Prefer avoiding `chmod 777`; configure ownership through pod-level `fsGroup` or remove the unused cache volume if persistence is not required.
````

This is not yet observable because the pod has never started. Prefer removing the unnecessary mount/init container for Memcached, or use storage permissions compatible with OpenShift’s arbitrary non-root UID model.

### Fix

**Step 1 — Delete the existing PVC:**
```
oc delete pvc memcached-data-pvc -n cache-tier
```

**Step 2 — Recreate with a valid StorageClass:**
```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: PersistentVolumeClaim
metadata:
  name: memcached-data-pvc
  namespace: cache-tier
  labels:
    app: memcached
spec:
  accessModes:
    - ReadWriteOnce
  resources:
    requests:
      storage: 2Gi
  storageClassName: gp3-csi
```

Once the PVC is bound, the pod will be scheduled and the Deployment will become available.

> **Note:** Memcached is an in-memory cache — using a PersistentVolume for `/cache` is unusual. If persistence isn't actually needed, an alternative fix is to change the Deployment's volume from a PVC to an `emptyDir`, which would eliminate the storage dependency entirely.

Would you like me to apply the fix?
````

````markdown
Cause: `NetworkPolicy` is blocking `frontend` from reaching `backend-service`.

Evidence
- `frontend` log in `service-mesh`:
  - `Connecting to backend-service:8080 (172.30.123.221:8080)`
  - `wget: download timed out`
- `backend-service` is correctly configured:
  - Service `service-mesh/backend-service`
  - ClusterIP `172.30.123.221`
  - Port `8080` → `targetPort: 8080`
- `backend-service` has a healthy endpoint:
  - Pod `service-mesh/backend-54d6d7b87c-l5gs4`
  - Endpoint IP `10.128.2.136:8080`
- `backend` pod is actually listening on `8080`:
  - `/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf` contains `listen 8080;`
  - `/proc/net/tcp` shows a listener on `0.0.0.0:8080`

Root cause
1. `NetworkPolicy service-mesh/backend-network-policy` selects the backend pods:
   - `podSelector: app=backend`
2. It only allows ingress from pods with:
   - `tier=backend`
3. The caller pod is:
   - `service-mesh/frontend-6cbbd65959-bglwr`
   - labels include `tier=frontend`

Relevant policy:
- `service-mesh/backend-network-policy`
- Ingress rule:
  - allows TCP `8080`
  - only from `podSelector.matchLabels.tier=backend`

So traffic from `frontend` is denied before it reaches the backend container. This is a Kubernetes/OVN network policy issue, not a service-mesh routing issue. I also found no Istio service-mesh routing/policy resources in this namespace during the check.

Fix
- Update `backend-network-policy` to allow `frontend` pods, for example by permitting `tier=frontend` or `app=frontend`.

Example mitigation:
```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: backend-network-policy
  namespace: service-mesh
spec:
  podSelector:
    matchLabels:
      app: backend
  policyTypes:
  - Ingress
  ingress:
  - from:
    - podSelector:
        matchLabels:
          app: frontend
    ports:
    - protocol: TCP
      port: 8080
```

Alternative
- If backend-to-backend traffic must also remain allowed, keep both sources:
  - `tier=backend`
  - `app=frontend`

Summary
- `backend-service` is up.
- `backend` is listening on `8080`.
- `frontend` resolves the service correctly.
- The blocking component is `NetworkPolicy service-mesh/backend-network-policy`.
````

I investigated `service-mesh` and the cause is a **NetworkPolicy blocking ingress to the backend**.

### Evidence

- `backend-service` exists and points to the backend pod:
  - Service: `service-mesh/backend-service`
  - ClusterIP: `172.30.123.221`
  - Port: `8080/TCP`
  - Endpoints: `10.128.2.136:8080`

- Both pods are running:
  - `frontend-6cbbd65959-bglwr`
  - `backend-54d6d7b87c-l5gs4`

- DNS from the frontend works:
  - `backend-service.service-mesh.svc.cluster.local -> 172.30.123.221`

- The backend is configured to listen on port `8080`:
  - `/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf: listen 8080;`

- Connectivity from frontend to backend fails:
  - Frontend log:
    - `Connecting to backend-service:8080 (172.30.123.221:8080)`
    - `wget: can't connect to remote host ... Connection refused`
    - later direct exec: `wget: download timed out`
  - Direct frontend -> backend pod IP also times out:
    - `wget http://10.128.2.136:8080` → `download timed out`

- The blocking policy is:
  - `service-mesh/backend-network-policy`

  ```yaml
  spec:
    podSelector:
      matchLabels:
        app: backend
    policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    ingress:
    - from:
      - podSelector:
          matchLabels:
            tier: backend
      ports:
      - port: 8080
        protocol: TCP
  ```

- Pod labels:
  - Backend pod: `app=backend`, `tier=backend`
  - Frontend pod: `app=frontend`, `tier=frontend`

### Root cause

`backend-network-policy` only allows ingress to pods with `app=backend` **from pods labeled `tier=backend`**.

The frontend pod is labeled `tier=frontend`, so its traffic to `backend-service:8080` is denied.

### Fix

Update `backend-network-policy` to allow frontend pods as a source. For example:

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: backend-network-policy
  namespace: service-mesh
spec:
  podSelector:
    matchLabels:
      app: backend
  policyTypes:
  - Ingress
  ingress:
  - from:
    - podSelector:
        matchLabels:
          app: frontend
    ports:
    - port: 8080
      protocol: TCP
```

If you want both backend-to-backend and frontend-to-backend traffic allowed, include both selectors in `from`.

### Conclusion

This is **not a Service or DNS problem**. The service resolves correctly and has endpoints. The failure is caused by the ingress `NetworkPolicy` on the backend.

If you want, I can update the policy for you.
````

            tier: backend
      ports:
      - port: 8080
        protocol: TCP
  ```

---

### Root Cause

The `backend-network-policy` NetworkPolicy isolates the `backend` pods and only permits ingress traffic from pods with the label `tier: backend`. 

Because the frontend pod carries the label `tier=frontend` (and `app=frontend`), its packets to `backend-service:8080` (`172.30.123.221:8080`) are dropped by the OVN-Kubernetes CNI, resulting in connection timeouts.

---

### Fix / Mitigation

Update `backend-network-policy` in namespace `service-mesh` to allow ingress traffic from the frontend pod by changing or adding the frontend label selector (`app: frontend` or `tier: frontend`):

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: backend-network-policy
  namespace: service-mesh
spec:
  podSelector:
    matchLabels:
      app: backend
  policyTypes:
  - Ingress
  ingress:
  - from:
    - podSelector:
        matchLabels:
          app: frontend
    ports:
    - protocol: TCP
      port: 8080
```
````

The discrepancy is real and comes from different workload definitions, not from failed or pending pods.

| Namespace | Running pods | Ready pods | Workloads |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `fleet-alpha` | **6** | **6** | 2 Deployments + 4 standalone Pods |
| `fleet-alpha1` | **9** | **9** | 9 Deployments |

### `fleet-alpha` — 6 pods

- Deployments:
  - `dispatcher-north` — 1/1
  - `dispatcher-south` — 1/1
- Standalone Pods:
  - `logger-central`
  - `scheduler-main`
  - `tracker-primary`
  - `tracker-secondary`

The four standalone pods have no owner references and were created directly as Pods. Their applied configuration confirms this—for example, `tracker-primary` was applied as a `kind: Pod`, not through a Deployment.

### `fleet-alpha1` — 9 pods

Each of these Deployments has one available replica:

- `cache-handler`
- `ingestion-node`
- `output-node`
- `proxy-router`
- `store-primary`

## unready_pod

Alert fires for a pod in not-ready state.
RCA: the readiness probe does an HTTP GET on port 9200 but the
container is a simple echo/sleep loop with no HTTP server.
Expected: remove the readiness probe or replace it with an exec
probe appropriate for the container.

**Tags**: `core`, `classic`, `difficulty_normal`, `alert`

### Query

```
The catalog indexing workload in discovery-hub never becomes available. Can you investigate the cause?
```

<a id="openai-gpt-5-4--unready_pod"></a>

### openai-gpt-5-4 (run 1/3)

**Correctness**: ✅ PASS (score: 0.98)

> Custom answer correctness: 0.98 - The response is essentially correct and fully aligned with the expected diagnosis. It clearly identifies the root cause: the pod never becomes Ready because the readiness probe performs an HTTP GET on `/healthz` at port `9200`, but the container is just running a `busybox` shell loop and does not serve HTTP on that port. That matches the expected response exactly.

**Duration**: 22s

**Tokens**: in 16,398 out 584

````markdown
Diagnosis: the workload is stuck unavailable because its readiness probe checks an HTTP endpoint that the container never serves.

Evidence
- Active alert: `DiscoveryHubPodNotReady` for pod `discovery-hub/catalog-index-service`.
- Pod status:
  - Pod: `catalog-index-service`
  - Namespace: `discovery-hub`
  - State: `Running`
  - Ready: `0/1`
- Pod event:
  - `Readiness probe failed: Get "http://10.128.2.153:9200/healthz": dial tcp 10.128.2.153:9200: connect: connection refused`
- Pod spec shows:
  - Image: `busybox:1.36`
  - Command: `sh -c while true; do echo 'Running...'; sleep 5; done`
  - Readiness probe: HTTP GET `:9200/healthz`
- Container logs only show:
  - `Running...`
- In-container process check:
  - Only process is the shell loop above.
- In-container listening sockets:
  - No TCP listener is present, so nothing is bound to port `9200`.

Root cause
1. The pod is not running a catalog index service at all.
   - It runs `busybox:1.36` with an infinite shell loop.
   - That process does not start an HTTP server.
2. The readiness probe expects an HTTP service on `port 9200`, path `/healthz`.
   - Since no process listens on `9200`, the probe always fails.
   - As a result, the pod never becomes Ready/Available.

Additional finding
- There is no `Deployment` or `StatefulSet` in `discovery-hub` for this workload; this is a standalone `Pod`.
- There is also no `Service` or `Route` in the namespace tied to it.

Fix
Use one of these:
1. If this should be a real catalog indexing service:
   - Replace the pod with the correct image and startup command that actually serves health on `9200/healthz`.
   - Best practice: run it as a `Deployment`, not a naked `Pod`.
2. If this pod is only meant to run a loop and not expose HTTP:
   - Remove or change the readiness probe to match the actual process behavior.
   - Example: use an `exec` probe or no readiness probe if appropriate.
