````markdown
- The current manifest is inconsistent: `busybox` + shell loop cannot satisfy an HTTP readiness probe.
````

````markdown
### Root Cause Diagnosis

The catalog indexing workload (`catalog-index-service` pod in namespace `discovery-hub`) is failing its **readiness probe**, preventing it from becoming available (`0/1` Ready).

#### Evidence
- **Pod Status**: Running, but container `indexer` is unready.
- **Readiness Probe Configuration**: 
  - Type: HTTP GET request to `http://:9200/healthz`.
  - Container Command: Runs a simple busybox loop (`sh -c "while true; do echo 'Running...'; sleep 5; done"`).
- **The Problem**: The container command only executes a shell loop printing text; **it does not start an HTTP server listening on port `9200`**. As a result, the readiness probe's HTTP requests to `/healthz` fail continuously, keeping the pod out of the endpoints list.

---

### Mitigation / Fix

To resolve this issue, the container application needs to be updated to actually serve the HTTP health endpoint on port `9200`, or the readiness probe needs to be corrected to match what the container provides (e.g., changing it to a TCP socket or `exec` command probe if an HTTP server is not being run).

Since this is a configuration/application logic mismatch, you can patch or recreate the pod/workload with a proper readiness check or a web server script. 

**Example Fix (Recreating/Updating the Pod with a dummy HTTP server using busybox `nc` or python):**
If you want to use a simple busybox HTTP server responding on port `9200`, you can update the container command to start a lightweight listener:
````

````markdown
## Root Cause: Readiness Probe Targets a Port That Nothing Is Listening On

The pod `catalog-index-service` in namespace `discovery-hub` is **Running but never becomes Ready** because its readiness probe is failing continuously.

### Evidence

1. **The container runs a simple shell loop — not an HTTP server:**
   ```
   command: ["sh", "-c", "while true; do echo 'Running...'; sleep 5; done"]
   ```
   This `busybox:1.36` container only prints "Running..." in a loop. It does **not** start any process that listens on port `9200`.

2. **The readiness probe expects an HTTP endpoint on port 9200:**
   ```yaml
   readinessProbe:
     httpGet:
       path: /healthz
       port: 9200
     initialDelaySeconds: 2
     periodSeconds: 4
     failureThreshold: 2
   ```

3. **Every probe attempt fails with "connection refused":**
   ```
   Readiness probe failed: Get "http://10.128.2.153:9200/healthz": dial tcp 10.128.2.153:9200: connect: connection refused
   ```
   This confirms nothing is listening on port 9200 inside the container.

4. **Pod status confirms the container is not ready:**
   - `ready: false`, `started: true`, `restartCount: 0`
   - Condition `ContainersReady: False` with reason `ContainersNotReady`

### Why It Will Never Become Available

The container image (`busybox:1.36`) and command do not include any HTTP server. Declaring `containerPort: 9200` in the spec only documents the intent — it does not actually open a port. Since no process binds to port 9200, the readiness probe will **never pass**, and the pod will **never** be marked Ready.

---

## Fix

You need to either:

1. **Use the correct container image** that runs the actual catalog indexing service with an HTTP health endpoint on port 9200 at `/healthz`. Replace `busybox:1.36` and the shell command with the proper application image.

2. **Or, if this is intentionally a lightweight container**, remove or adjust the readiness probe to match what the container actually does. For example, use an `exec` probe that checks the process is running:
   ```yaml
   readinessProbe:
     exec:
       command: ["sh", "-c", "pgrep -f 'sleep'"]
     initialDelaySeconds: 2
     periodSeconds: 4
   ```

**Option 1 is the recommended fix** — the pod spec appears to be misconfigured with a placeholder image/command instead of the real catalog indexing application.
````

````markdown
The pod `catalog-index-service` in namespace `discovery-hub` is **Running but never becomes Ready** because of a fundamental mismatch between what the container does and what the readiness probe expects.

### Evidence

1. **Container command** — The `indexer` container runs a simple shell loop that does nothing but print "Running..." every 5 seconds:

   ```

### Fix
````

````markdown
## Root Cause: Readiness Probe Targets a Port That Nothing Is Listening On

The pod `catalog-index-service` in namespace `discovery-hub` will **never** become ready because of a fundamental mismatch between the readiness probe configuration and the container's actual behavior.

### Evidence

1. **Readiness probe configuration** — The pod defines an HTTP readiness probe:

   readinessProbe:
````
